Further study of the maximally symmetry breaking patterns in an SU ⁡ ( 8 ) {\rm SU}(8) theory
Ning Chen, Zhiyuan Chen, Zhanpeng Hou, Zhaolong Teng, Bin Wang

Abstract
An SU ⁡ ( 8 ) {\rm SU}(8) theory was previously found to be the minimal simple gauge group where all three-generational Standard Model (SM) fermions can be nontrivially embedded. It is maximally broken into a subgroup of SU ⁡ ( 8 ) → 𝒢 441 ≡ SU ​ ( 4 ) s ⊗ SU ​ ( 4 ) W ⊗ U ​ ( 1 ) X 0 {\rm SU}(8)\to\mathcal{G}_{441}\equiv{\rm SU}(4)_{s}\otimes{\rm SU}(4)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{X_{0}} at the grand unified theory scale by the SU ⁡ ( 8 ) {\rm SU}(8) adjoint Higgs field of 𝟔𝟑 𝐇 \mathbf{63_{H}} . Gauge symmetries in the strong and the weak sectors are extended by one and two ranks, respectively. The sequential strong-weak-weak (SWW) symmetry breaking stages were found to generate the observed hierarchical SM quark/lepton masses as well as the Cabibbo-Kobayashi-Maskawa mixing pattern with the precise flavor identifications [ 17 , 20 ] . We further study the possible weak-strong-weak and weak-weak-strong symmetry breaking patterns, and compare with the results that we have obtained by following the SWW sequence. The two-loop renormalization group equations following both patterns are analyzed, where we cannot achieve the gauge coupling unification in the field theory framework. Through these analyses, we suggest the gauge coupling unification to be interpreted in the context of the affine Lie algebra.

1
Introduction

The grand unified theory (GUT) was proposed to unify all three fundamental symmetries described by the Standard Model (SM).
Two original versions that have been widely studied over decades are the
\rm SU(5)
Georgi-Glashow theory
[
1
]
and the
\rm SO(10)
Fritzsch-Minkowski theory
[
2
]
, as well as their supersymmetric extensions
[
3
]
.
However, the minimal unified frameworks cannot address much on the existing flavor puzzles of all three-generational SM fermions, unless further ingredients are included
[
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
,
11
]
.

From the experimental perspective, the indispensable guidance to unveil the flavor puzzle (particularly for quarks and leptons) to date is the discovery
[
12
,
13
]
and the measurements
[
14
,
15
]
of one single SM Higgs boson.
The LHC measurements have confirmed that the Yukawa couplings at the electroweak (EW) scale between the SM Higgs boson and the
(t\,,b\,,\tau\,,\mu)
are consistent with the SM predictions.
Based on the experimental facts, one natural conjecture is that the hierarchical Yukawa couplings of the SM Higgs boson originate from the flavor nonuniversalities to all three generations beyond the SM.
Obviously, the minimal
\rm SU(5)
or
\rm SO(10)
theories only allow the simply repetitive flavor structure.
In an earlier study by Georgi
[
16
]
, he proposed to extend the gauge group beyond the minimal
{\rm SU}(5)
so that three-generational SM fermions can be nontrivially embedded.
Consequently, three-generational SM fermions must transform differently in the UV-complete theory.
In the original third law of the
\rm SU(N)
unified theory, Georgi determined to have nonrepetitive
{\rm SU}(N)
irreducible representations (IRs), which lead to a minimal three-generational
{\rm SU}(11)
framework with the chiral fermions of

\displaystyle\{f_{L}\}_{{\rm SU}(11)}^{n_{g}=3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[11\,,4\right]_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus\left[11\,,8\right]_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus\left[11\,,9\right]_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus\left[11\,,10\right]_{\mathbf{F}}\,.

(1)

In recent studies, we introduced a concept of chiral irreducible anomaly-free fermion sets (IRAFFSs), which reads
[
17
]
:

Definition
.

A chiral IRAFFS is a set of left-handed antisymmetric fermions of
\sum_{\mathcal{R}}m_{\mathcal{R}}\,\mathcal{F}_{L}(\mathcal{R})
, with
m_{\mathcal{R}}
being the multiplicities of a particular fermion representation of
\mathcal{R}
.
Obviously, the anomaly-free condition reads
\sum_{\mathcal{R}}m_{\mathcal{R}}\,{\rm Anom}(\mathcal{F}_{L}(\mathcal{R}))=0
.
We also require the following conditions to be satisfied for a chiral IRAFFS:

•

the greatest common divisor (GCD) of the
\{m_{\mathcal{R}}\}
should satisfy that
{\rm GCD}\{m_{\mathcal{R}}\}=1
;

•

the fermions in a chiral IRAFFS can no longer be removed, which would otherwise bring nonvanishing gauge anomalies;

•

there should not be any singlet, self-conjugate, adjoint fermions, or vectorial fermion pairs in a chiral IRAFFS.

Correspondingly, Georgi’s 1979 third law
[
16
]
can be reformulated as follows
[
17
]
.

Conjecture
(Chen).

Only distinctive chiral IRAFFSs are allowed in the GUT.

This leads to an
{\rm SU}(8)
theory with the minimal chiral fermions of

\displaystyle\{f_{L}\}_{{\rm SU}(8)}^{n_{g}=3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\omega}\oplus\mathbf{28_{F}}\Big]\bigoplus\Big[\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}}\oplus\mathbf{56_{F}}\Big]\,,\penalty\ {\rm dim}_{\mathbf{F}}=156\,,

(2)

\displaystyle\Omega\equiv(\omega\,,\dot{\omega})\,,\penalty\ \omega=(3\,,{\rm IV}\,,{\rm V}\,,{\rm VI})\,,\penalty\ \dot{\omega}=(\dot{1}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm VII}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}\,,\dot{\rm IX})\,,

with undotted/dotted indices for the
\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}
’s in the rank-two chiral IRAFFS and the rank-three chiral IRAFFS, respectively.
The Roman numbers and the Arabic numbers are used for the heavy partner fermions and the SM fermions.
The same chiral fermions in Eq. (
2
) were previously proposed in Ref.
[
18
]
, while a partition based on the chiral IRAFFSs was not acknowledged.

The
{\rm SU}(8)
gauge group has rank seven, which predicts three intermediate symmetry breaking stages between the GUT scale and the EW scale.
The adjoint Higgs field of
\mathbf{63_{H}}
achieves the maximally breaking pattern of
{\rm SU}(8)\to\mathcal{G}_{441}
through its vacuum expectation value (VEV) of

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{63_{H}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}{\rm diag}(-\mathbb{I}_{4\times 4}\,,+\mathbb{I}_{4\times 4})v_{U}\,,

(3)

according to Li
[
19
]
.
The
{\rm U}(1)_{X_{0}}
charges of the
{\rm SU}(8)
fundamental representation are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{0}(\mathbf{8})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm diag}(\underbrace{-\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{I}_{4\times 4}}_{\mathbf{4_{s}}}\,,\underbrace{+\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{I}_{4\times 4}}_{\mathbf{4_{W}}})\,.

(4)

In this pattern, the gauge symmetries in the strong and the weak sectors are extended by one and two ranks beyond the SM gauge groups, respectively.
The sequential symmetry breaking patterns may follow the strong-weak-weak (SWW)
[
17
,
20
,
21
]
, the weak-strong-weak (WSW), and the weak-weak-strong (WWS) sequences.
These cannot be determined purely by group theory.
Other different symmetry breaking patterns of the
{\rm SU}(8)
group were previously described in Refs.
[
22
,
23
,
24
,
25
,
26
,
27
]
.

Through the previous analyses of the SWW symmetry breaking pattern
[
17
,
20
]
, we found (i) one unique SM Higgs boson responsible for the electroweak symmetry breaking (EWSB) by looking for the origin of the global
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{B-L}
symmetry, (ii) the hierarchical Yukawa couplings to all three-generational SM quarks/leptons due to the intrinsic symmetry properties, and (iii) the reasonable Cabibbo-Kobayashi-Maskawa (CKM) mixing pattern
[
28
,
29
]
in the quark sector.
Three intermediate symmetry breaking scales beyond the EW scale enter into the SM quark/lepton mass matrices, and hence are set by requiring a reasonable matching with the experimental measurements.
Meanwhile, the renormalization group equations (RGEs) of three gauge couplings in the SWW symmetry breaking pattern together with three intermediate scales could not achieve the unification in the field theory context
[
21
]
.
In this work, we further analyze two other possible WSW and WWS symmetry breaking patterns of the
{\rm SU}(8)
theory, where the extended weak symmetries breaking pattern of
\mathcal{G}_{441}\to\mathcal{G}_{431}

1
1

1

An effective
\mathcal{G}_{431}
was previously studied in Ref.
[
30
]
.
happened at the first stage.
Within each symmetry breaking pattern, we shall look for whether the reported hierarchical SM quark/lepton masses as well as the CKM mixing patterns following the SWW sequence in Ref.
[
20
]
can be reproduced.
Furthermore, we shall derive the RGEs in both the WSW and the WWS symmetry breaking patterns to look for the gauge coupling unification.

The rest of the paper is organized as follows.
In Sec.
2
, we review the
{\rm SU}(8)
framework, where three-generational SM fermions are nontrivially embedded, and hence they transform differently in the UV theory.
We focus on the WSW and the WWS symmetry breaking patterns, define various gauge
{\rm U}(1)
and nonanomalous global
\widetilde{\rm U}(1)_{T}
charges at different stages, and decompose all
{\rm SU}(8)
chiral fermions and Higgs fields accordingly.
A set of
d=5
operators that generate all light SM quark/lepton mass terms other than the top quark mass are collected according to Ref.
[
20
]
.
In Secs.
3
and
4
, we collect the vectorlike fermion masses and the benchmark points in both the WSW and the WWS symmetry breaking patterns.
In Sec.
5
, we obtain the RGEs of the minimal
{\rm SU}(8)
theory by following both WSW and WWS sequences based on the suggested benchmark points.
The RGE behaviors in none of the symmetry breaking patterns are likely to achieve the conventional gauge coupling unification, and their behaviors also match with what we have found in the SWW sequence
[
21
]
by and large.
We conclude and make future perspective in Sec.
6
, where we suggest that the gauge coupling unification in the minimal
{\rm SU}(8)
theory to be interpreted in terms of the affine Lie algebra.
In Appendix
A
, we collect the SM quark/lepton mass matrices from the SWW symmetry breaking pattern that we have obtained in Refs.
[
17
,
20
]
.
In Appendixes
B
and
C
, we present details in both WSW and WWS symmetry breaking patterns, where we describe the procedures to integrate out the massive vectorlike fermions, and also derive the SM quark/lepton mass terms based on a set of
d=5
fermion bilinear operators and the irreducible Higgs mixing operators.
In order to reproduce the SM quark/lepton mass hierarchies and the CKM mixing pattern in Ref.
[
20
]
, the SM flavor identifications are modified for the first and second generations accordingly..

2
The
{\rm SU}(8)
theory and possible symmetry breaking patterns

2.1
Overview

The
{\rm SU}(8)
theory was formulated by requiring several distinctive chiral IRAFFSs that can lead to three-generational SM fermions at the electroweak scale
[
17
,
20
]
.
The nonanomalous global Dimopolous-Raby-Susskind (DRS) symmetries
[
31
]
from fermions in Eq. (
2
) are

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\rm DRS}\left[{\rm SU}(8)\,,n_{g}=3\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\widetilde{{\rm SU}}(4)_{\omega}\otimes\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T_{2}}\Big]\bigotimes\Big[\widetilde{{\rm SU}}(5)_{\dot{\omega}}\otimes\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T_{3}}\Big]\,,

(5)

and we also denote the anomalous global Peccei-Quinn (PQ) symmetries
[
32
]
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\rm PQ}\left[{\rm SU}(8)\,,n_{g}=3\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{{\rm PQ}_{2}}\bigotimes\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{{\rm PQ}_{3}}\,.

(6)

Since the global
B-L
symmetry should be identical for all three generations, we further require a common nonanomalous
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T}\equiv\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T_{2}}=\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T_{3}}
between two chiral IRAFFSs.
The anomalous global
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{\rm PQ}
charges are assigned such that

\displaystyle p:q_{2}\neq-3:+2\,,\quad p:q_{3}\neq-3:+1\,.

(7)

\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{28_{H}}+{\rm H.c.}

[

. Instead, only a
d=5

\frac{1}{M_{\rm pl}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{63_{H}}

\mathbf{56_{F}}

\widetilde{{\rm SU}}(5)_{\dot{\omega}}

\widetilde{\rm U}(1)_{\rm PQ}

p+3q_{3}\neq 0
from Eq. (
7
), it is only possible due to the gravitational effect.

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}_{Y}

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\omega}\mathbf{28_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}+Y_{\mathcal{T}}\mathbf{28_{F}}\mathbf{28_{F}}\mathbf{70_{H}}

(8)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}+\frac{c_{4}}{M_{\rm pl}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{63_{H}}+H.c.\,,

with the reduced Planck scale of
M_{\rm pl}=(8\pi G_{N})^{-1/2}=2.4\times 10^{18}\,{\rm GeV}
.
All renormalizable Yukawa couplings and nonrenormalizable Wilson coefficients are expected to be
(Y_{\mathcal{B}}\,,Y_{\mathcal{T}}\,,Y_{\mathcal{D}}\,,c_{4})\sim\mathcal{O}(1)
.
Altogether, we collect the
{\rm SU}(8)
Higgs fields as follows:

\displaystyle\{H\}_{{\rm SU}(8)}^{n_{g}=3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}\oplus\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus\mathbf{70_{H}}\oplus\underline{\mathbf{63_{H}}}\,,\penalty\ {\rm dim}_{\mathbf{H}}=547\,,

(9)

where the adjoint Higgs field of
\mathbf{63_{H}}
is real, while all others are complex.
Accordingly, the non-anomalous global
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T}
charges and the anomalous global
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{\rm PQ}
charges for all fermions and Higgs fields are assigned in Table
1
.

2.2
The SWW symmetry breaking pattern

The SWW symmetry breaking pattern of the
{\rm SU}(8)
theory follows the sequence of

\displaystyle{\rm SU}(8)\xrightarrow{v_{U}}\mathcal{G}_{441}\xrightarrow{v_{441}}\mathcal{G}_{341}\xrightarrow{v_{341}}\mathcal{G}_{331}\xrightarrow{v_{331}}\mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}\xrightarrow{v_{\rm EW}}{\rm SU}(3)_{c}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{\rm EM}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{441}\equiv{\rm SU}(4)_{s}\otimes{\rm SU}(4)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{X_{0}}\,,\penalty\ \mathcal{G}_{341}\equiv{\rm SU}(3)_{c}\otimes{\rm SU}(4)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{X_{1}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{331}\equiv{\rm SU}(3)_{c}\otimes{\rm SU}(3)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{X_{2}}\,,\penalty\ \mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}\equiv{\rm SU}(3)_{c}\otimes{\rm SU}(2)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{Y}\,,

\displaystyle\textrm{with}\penalty\ v_{U}\gg v_{441}\gg v_{341}\gg v_{331}\gg v_{\rm EW}\,,

(10)

which was previously studied in Refs.
[
17
,
20
,
21
]
.
Once one assumes that the extended gauge symmetries in the strong sector break first, there is no ambiguity of the sequential symmetry breaking patterns.
Sequentially, the
{\rm U}(1)_{X_{1}}
,
{\rm U}(1)_{X_{2}}
, and
{\rm U}(1)_{Y}
charges are defined according to the
{\rm SU}(4)_{s}
and the
{\rm SU}(4)_{W}
fundamental representations as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{1}(\mathbf{4_{s}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big(\underbrace{(-\frac{1}{12}+\mathcal{X}_{0})\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3}}_{\mathbf{3_{c}}}\,,\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{0}\Big)\,,

(11a)

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{2}(\mathbf{4_{W}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big(\underbrace{(\frac{1}{12}+\mathcal{X}_{1})\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3}}_{\mathbf{3_{W}}}\,,-\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{1}\Big)\,,

(11b)

\displaystyle\hat{Y}(\mathbf{4_{W}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big((\frac{1}{6}+\mathcal{X}_{2})\mathbb{I}_{2\times 2}\,,-\frac{1}{3}+\mathcal{X}_{2}\,,\mathcal{X}_{2}\Big)

(11c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big(\underbrace{(\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{0})\mathbb{I}_{2\times 2}}_{\mathbf{2_{W}}}\,,(-\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{0})\mathbb{I}_{2\times 2}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{Q}_{e}(\mathbf{4_{W}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle T_{{\rm SU}(4)}^{3}+\hat{Y}(\mathbf{4_{W}})={\rm diag}\,\Big(\frac{3}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{1}\,,(-\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{1})\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3}\Big)\,.

(11d)

The nonanomalous global
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T}
symmetry becomes the global
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{B-L}
at the EW scale according to the following sequence
[
17
]

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{441}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{T}^{\prime}\equiv\mathcal{T}-4t\mathcal{X}_{0}\,,\quad\mathcal{G}_{341}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\prime}+8t\mathcal{X}_{1}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{331}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}\,,\quad\mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{B}-\mathcal{L}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}\,.

(12)

2.3
The WSW symmetry breaking pattern

The WSW symmetry breaking pattern of the
{\rm SU}(8)
theory follows the sequence of

\displaystyle{\rm SU}(8)\xrightarrow{v_{U}}\mathcal{G}_{441}\xrightarrow{v_{441}}\mathcal{G}_{431}\xrightarrow{v_{431}}\mathcal{G}_{331}\xrightarrow{v_{331}}\mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}\xrightarrow{v_{\rm EW}}{\rm SU}(3)_{c}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{\rm EM}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{441}\equiv{\rm SU}(4)_{s}\otimes{\rm SU}(4)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{X_{0}}\,,\penalty\ \mathcal{G}_{431}\equiv{\rm SU}(4)_{c}\otimes{\rm SU}(3)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{X_{1}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{331}\equiv{\rm SU}(3)_{c}\otimes{\rm SU}(3)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{X_{2}}\,,\penalty\ \mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}\equiv{\rm SU}(3)_{c}\otimes{\rm SU}(2)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{Y}\,,

\displaystyle\textrm{with}\penalty\ v_{U}\gg v_{441}\gg v_{431}\gg v_{331}\gg v_{\rm EW}\,.

(13)

The
{\rm U}(1)_{X_{1}}
,
{\rm U}(1)_{X_{2}}
, and
{\rm U}(1)_{Y}
charges along the WSW sequence are defined according to the
{\rm SU}(4)_{s}
and the
{\rm SU}(4)_{W}
fundamental representations as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{1}(\mathbf{4_{W}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big(\underbrace{(\frac{1}{12}+\mathcal{X}_{0})\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3}}_{\mathbf{3_{W}}}\,,-\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{0}\Big)\,,

(14a)

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{2}(\mathbf{4_{s}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big(\underbrace{(-\frac{1}{12}+\mathcal{X}_{1})\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3}}_{\mathbf{3_{c}}}\,,\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{1}\Big)\,,

(14b)

\displaystyle\hat{Y}(\mathbf{4_{W}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big((\frac{1}{6}+\mathcal{X}_{2})\mathbb{I}_{2\times 2}\,,-\frac{1}{3}+\mathcal{X}_{2}\,,\mathcal{X}_{2}\Big)

(14c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big(\underbrace{(\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{0})\mathbb{I}_{2\times 2}}_{\mathbf{2_{W}}}\,,(-\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{0})\mathbb{I}_{2\times 2}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{Q}_{e}(\mathbf{4_{W}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle T_{{\rm SU}(4)}^{3}+\hat{Y}(\mathbf{4_{W}})={\rm diag}\,\Big(\frac{3}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{0}\,,(-\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{0})\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3}\Big)\,.

(14d)

We decompose the Higgs fields in the Yukawa term into components that can be responsible for the sequential symmetry breaking pattern in Eq. (
13
).
All possible Higgs components that are likely to develop VEVs for the corresponding symmetry breaking stages are marked by
\langle...\rangle
, while their UV origins are denoted by underlines.

\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}

(20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{441}\to\mathcal{G}_{431}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,{\rm IV}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}W_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,{\rm IV}}\,,

(22b)

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,3,\rm{VI}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,3,\rm{VI}}\,,

(22c)

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm IX}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{\rm IX}}\,,\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm VIII}}^{\prime}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm VIII}}^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle{\rm EWSB}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

(22d)

For our later convenience, we also parametrize different symmetry breaking VEVs,

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}\equiv\frac{v_{U}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\zeta_{1}\equiv\frac{W_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,{\rm IV}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\zeta_{2}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,{\rm V}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{2}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{3}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,3,{\rm VI}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm VIII}}^{\prime}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{3}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{\rm IX}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}\gg\zeta_{1}\gg\zeta_{2}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{2}\gg\zeta_{3}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}\,,\quad\zeta_{ij}\equiv\frac{\zeta_{j}}{\zeta_{i}}\,,\quad(i<j)\,,

(23)

in terms of dimensionless quantities.
Here, we adopt the following conventions:

(i)

The notations of
(W\,,w\,,V)
and the dimensionless quantities of
(\zeta_{1}\,,\zeta_{2}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{2}\,,\zeta_{3}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3})
are used for the Higgs VEVs at the first, second, and third symmetry breaking stages, regardless of the specific symmetry breaking patterns.

(ii)

W_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}}
and
w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}}
represent the VEVs for the
\mathcal{G}_{441}\to\mathcal{G}_{431}
and the
\mathcal{G}_{431}\to\mathcal{G}_{331}
stages in Eqs. (
22
).
In the SWW sequence
[
20
]
,
W_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}}
and
w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}}
represent the VEVs for the
\mathcal{G}_{441}\to\mathcal{G}_{341}
and the
\mathcal{G}_{341}\to\mathcal{G}_{331}
stages instead.

2.4
The WWS symmetry breaking pattern

The WWS symmetry breaking pattern of the
{\rm SU}(8)
theory follows the sequence of

\displaystyle{\rm SU}(8)\xrightarrow{v_{U}}\mathcal{G}_{441}\xrightarrow{v_{441}}\mathcal{G}_{431}\xrightarrow{v_{431}}\mathcal{G}_{421}\xrightarrow{v_{421}}\mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}\xrightarrow{v_{\rm EW}}{\rm SU}(3)_{c}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{\rm EM}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{441}\equiv{\rm SU}(4)_{s}\otimes{\rm SU}(4)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{X_{0}}\,,\penalty\ \mathcal{G}_{431}\equiv{\rm SU}(4)_{s}\otimes{\rm SU}(3)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{X_{1}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{421}\equiv{\rm SU}(4)_{s}\otimes{\rm SU}(2)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{X_{2}}\,,\penalty\ \mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}\equiv{\rm SU}(3)_{c}\otimes{\rm SU}(2)_{W}\otimes{\rm U}(1)_{Y}\,,

\displaystyle\textrm{with}\penalty\ v_{U}\gg v_{441}\gg v_{431}\gg v_{331}\gg v_{\rm EW}\,.

(24)

The
{\rm U}(1)_{X_{1}}
,
{\rm U}(1)_{X_{2}}
, and
{\rm U}(1)_{Y}
charges along the WWS sequence are defined according to the
{\rm SU}(4)_{s}
and the
{\rm SU}(4)_{W}
fundamental representations as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{1}(\mathbf{4_{W}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big(\underbrace{(\frac{1}{12}+\mathcal{X}_{0})\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3}}_{\mathbf{3_{W}}}\,,-\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{0}\Big)\,,

(25a)

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{2}(\mathbf{3_{W}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big(\underbrace{(\frac{1}{6}+\mathcal{X}_{1})\mathbb{I}_{2\times 2}}_{\mathbf{2_{W}}}\,,-\frac{1}{3}+\mathcal{X}_{1}\Big)\,,

(25b)

\displaystyle\hat{Y}(\mathbf{4_{s}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\,\Big(\underbrace{(-\frac{1}{12}+\mathcal{X}_{2})\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3}}_{\mathbf{3_{c}}}\,,\frac{1}{4}+\mathcal{X}_{2}\Big)\,.

(25c)

The nonanomalous global
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T}
symmetry becomes the global
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{B-L}
at the EW scale according to the following sequence
[
17
]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{441}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{T}^{\prime}\equiv\mathcal{T}+4t\mathcal{X}_{0}\,,\quad\mathcal{G}_{431}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{421}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}-8t\mathcal{X}_{2}\,,\quad\mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{B}-\mathcal{L}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}+8t\mathcal{Y}\,.

(26)

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{N}}_{L}^{{\rm IV}}\equiv\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{L}^{\prime\prime}\,,\penalty\ \check{\mathcal{N}}_{L}^{{\rm IV}^{\prime}}\equiv\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{L}\,,\penalty\ \check{\mathcal{N}}_{L}^{{\rm V}^{\prime}}\equiv\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{L}^{\prime}\,,\penalty\ \check{\mathcal{N}}_{L}^{\dot{\rm VII}^{\prime}}\equiv\check{\mathfrak{n}}_{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}\,.

are exchanged from what we have obtained in the SWW pattern.
The detailed derivation of the corresponding SM quark/lepton mass matrices will be elaborated in Sec.

.

We decompose the Higgs fields in the Yukawa term into components that can be responsible for the sequential symmetry breaking pattern in Eq. (
24
).
For Higgs fields of
\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}
they read

\displaystyle\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}}\oplus\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\rangle

(30)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}}\oplus\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\rangle

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\rangle\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}}\supset\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\rangle\,.

\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}

(31)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\left[\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\rangle\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\right]\oplus\left[\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\oplus\left[\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\rangle\oplus\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\rangle\right]

\mathbf{70_{H}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{70_{H}}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-1)_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{4}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}}

(32)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{H}}}\supset\underline{(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}}}\supset\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\rangle\,.

Schematically, we assign the Higgs VEVs according to the symmetry breaking pattern as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{441}\to\mathcal{G}_{431}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,{\rm IV}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}W_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,{\rm IV}}\,,

(33a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{431}\to\mathcal{G}_{421}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,{\rm V}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,{\rm V}}\,,\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}^{\prime}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}\,,

(33b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{421}\to\mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,3,\rm{VI}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,3,\rm{VI}}\,,

(33c)

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm IX}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm IX}}\,,\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}^{\prime}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle{\rm EWSB}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}v_{\rm EW}\,.

(33d)

At the third stage, the Higgs VEVs from the rank-three sector are due to the components of
(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm IX}}\subset(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm IX}}
and
(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}^{\prime}\subset(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}
.
This is different from the third stage of the WSW pattern, where the Higgs VEVs from the rank-three sector are from different IRs of
(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}
and
(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm IX}}
in Eq. (
22c
), respectively.
For our later convenience, we also parametrize different symmetry breaking VEVs,

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}\equiv\frac{v_{U}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\zeta_{1}\equiv\frac{W_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,{\rm IV}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\zeta_{2}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,3,{\rm V}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{2}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{3}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,{\rm VI}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}^{\prime}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{3}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm IX}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}\gg\zeta_{1}\gg\zeta_{2}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{2}\gg\zeta_{3}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}\,,\quad\zeta_{ij}\equiv\frac{\zeta_{j}}{\zeta_{i}}\,,\quad(i<j)\,,

(34)

in terms of dimensionless quantities.
Notice that we use the same notations as in Eq. (
23
) to manifest the intrinsic hierarchies, while their symmetry breaking patterns should be distinguishable.

\displaystyle c_{3}\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}^{(3\,,2)}

\displaystyle d_{\mathscr{A}}\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{A}}^{d=5}

3.2
The CKM matrix and the benchmark

The biunitary transformations of

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{L}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{R}^{\dagger}=\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm diag}\,,\quad\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\dagger}=\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{L}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm diag})^{2}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{L}\,,\quad\mathcal{F}=(\mathcal{U}\,,\mathcal{D})\,,

(37)

diagonalize the unhatted flavor eigenstates into their hatted mass eigenstates.
To obtain the CKM matrix of the quark sector, we derive the left-handed mixing matrices of
(\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}\,,\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L})
of

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\hat{u}_{L}\\
\hat{c}_{L}\\
\hat{t}_{L}\\
\end{array}\right)=\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{L}\\
c_{L}\\
t_{L}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\hat{d}_{L}\\
\hat{s}_{L}\\
\hat{b}_{L}\\
\end{array}\right)=\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L}\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d_{L}\\
s_{L}\\
b_{L}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

through their perturbative expansions in Eqs. (
162
) and (
172
).
Explicitly, we find that

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}=\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(12)}\cdot\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(23)}\cdot\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(13)}\cdot\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{ID}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{ID}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&1&0\\
-1&0&0\\
0&0&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(12)}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\cos{\epsilon_1}&-\sin{\epsilon_1}&0\\
\sin{\epsilon_1}&\cos{\epsilon_1}&0\\
0&0&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\sin{\epsilon_1}\simeq\frac{m_{u}}{m_{c}}\frac{\zeta_{1}}{\zeta_{2}}-\frac{\zeta_{2}}{\zeta_{1}}\sim\mathcal{O}(10^{-2}),

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(13)}\approx\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1&0&-\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{2}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}\\
0&1&0\\
\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{2}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}&0&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(23)}\approx\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1&0&0\\
0&1&\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{1}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}\\
0&-\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{1}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

and

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\sin\lambda&\cos\lambda&\\
-\cos\lambda&\sin\lambda&\\
&&1\\
\end{array}\right)\approx\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\lambda&1-\lambda^{2}/2&\\
-1+\lambda^{2}/2&\lambda&\\
&&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,.

The CKM matrix can be approximated as the Wolfenstein parametrization

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{\rm CKM}\Big|_{{\rm SU}(8)\,,{\rm WSW}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L}^{\dagger}\approx\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1-\lambda^{2}/2&\lambda&-c_{5}\zeta_{2}/Y_{\mathcal{T}}\\
-\lambda&1-\lambda^{2}/2&c_{5}\zeta_{1}/Y_{\mathcal{T}}\\
c_{5}(\lambda\zeta_{1}+\zeta_{2})/Y_{\mathcal{T}}&-c_{5}\zeta_{1}/Y_{\mathcal{T}}&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

which is identical to what we have obtained in the SWW sequence
[
20
]
.
For completeness, we tabulate the benchmark point of the
{\rm SU}(8)
along the WSW sequence, as well as the predicted SM quantities in Table
9
.
Three dimensionless parameters of
(\zeta_{1}\,,\zeta_{2}\,,\zeta_{3})
can be translated into three intermediate symmetry breaking scales in Eq. (
13
) as follows:

\displaystyle v_{441}\simeq 1.4\times 10^{17}\,{\rm GeV}\,,\quad v_{431}\simeq 4.8\times 10^{15}\,{\rm GeV}\,,\quad v_{331}\simeq 4.8\times 10^{13}\,{\rm GeV}\,.

(76)

4.2
The CKM matrix and the benchmark

The biunitary transformations of

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{L}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{R}^{\dagger}=\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm diag}\,,\quad\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\dagger}=\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{L}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm diag})^{2}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{L}\,,\quad\mathcal{F}=(\mathcal{U}\,,\mathcal{D})\,,

(77)

diagonalize the unhatted flavor eigenstates into their hatted mass eigenstates.
To obtain the CKM matrix of the quark sector, we derive the left-handed mixing matrices of
(\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}\,,\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L})
of

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\hat{u}_{L}\\
\hat{c}_{L}\\
\hat{t}_{L}\\
\end{array}\right)=\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{L}\\
c_{L}\\
t_{L}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\hat{d}_{L}\\
\hat{s}_{L}\\
\hat{b}_{L}\\
\end{array}\right)=\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L}\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d_{L}\\
s_{L}\\
b_{L}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

through their perturbative expansions in Eqs. (
162
) and (
172
).
Explicitly, we find that

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}=\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(12)}\cdot\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(23)}\cdot\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(13)}\cdot\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{ID}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{ID}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&1&0\\
-1&0&0\\
0&0&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(12)}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\cos{\epsilon_2}&-\sin{\epsilon_2}&0\\
\sin{\epsilon_2}&\cos{\epsilon_2}&0\\
0&0&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\sin{\epsilon_2}\simeq\frac{m_{u}}{m_{c}}\frac{\zeta_{1}}{\zeta_{3}}-\frac{\zeta_{3}}{\zeta_{1}}\sim\mathcal{O}(\zeta_{13}),

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(13)}\approx\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1&0&-\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{3}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}\\
0&1&0\\
\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{3}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}&0&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}^{(23)}\approx\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1&0&0\\
0&1&\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{1}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}\\
0&-\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{1}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

and

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L}\approx\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\zeta_{23}&1-\zeta_{23}^{2}/2&0\\
-1+\zeta_{23}^{2}/2&\zeta_{23}&0\\
0&0&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,.

The CKM matrix can be approximated as the Wolfenstein parametrization

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{\rm CKM}\Big|_{{\rm SU}(8)\,,{\rm WWS}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{L}\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L}^{\dagger}\approx\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1-\zeta_{23}^{2}/2&\zeta_{23}&-\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{3}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}\\
-\zeta_{23}&1-\zeta_{23}^{2}/2&\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{1}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}\\
\frac{c_{5}\left(\zeta_{23}\zeta_{1}+\zeta_{3}\right)}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}&-\frac{c_{5}\zeta_{1}}{\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}&1\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

where the Cabibbo mixing parameter is interpreted as the ratio of
\lambda=|V_{us}|=\zeta_{23}
.
The observed mixing hierarchy of
|V_{cb}|\gg|V_{ub}|
is due to the hierarchy of
\zeta_{1}\gg\zeta_{3}
, while this was due to the hierarchy of
\zeta_{1}\gg\zeta_{2}
in the SWW and the WSW patterns.
To obtain the reasonable CKM mixing parameters, one has to choose a suppressed
c_{3}=0.01
for the reasonable
(d\,,e)
masses, and a relatively enhanced Higgs mixing parameter of
d_{\mathscr{A}}=0.2
for the reasonable
(b\,,\tau)
masses.

) as follows:

5
The gauge coupling evolutions in the SU(8) theory

The gauge coupling evolutions rely on the RGEs of the
{\rm SU}(8)
theory.
The two-loop RGE of a gauge coupling of
\alpha_{\Upsilon}
in the
\overline{\rm MS}
scheme is given by
[
33
]

\displaystyle\frac{d\alpha_{\Upsilon}(\mu)}{d\log\mu}=\frac{b_{\Upsilon}^{(1)}}{2\pi}\alpha_{\Upsilon}^{2}(\mu)+\Big(\sum_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}\frac{b_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}^{(2)}}{8\pi^{2}}\alpha_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}(\mu)+\sum_{\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{T}\,,\mathcal{B}\,,\mathcal{D}}f_{\Upsilon}(Y_{\mathcal{F}})\Big)\cdot\alpha_{\Upsilon}^{2}(\mu)\,,

(114)

where the one- and two-loop
\beta
coefficients for the non-Abelian groups are

\displaystyle b^{\left(1\right)}_{\Upsilon}=

\displaystyle-\frac{11}{3}C_{2}\left(\mathcal{G}_{\Upsilon}\right)+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{F}T\left(\mathcal{R}^{F}_{\Upsilon}\right)+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{S}T\left(\mathcal{R}^{S}_{\Upsilon}\right)\,,

(115a)

\displaystyle b^{\left(2\right)}_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{34}{3}C_{2}\left(\mathcal{G}_{\Upsilon}\right)^{2}+\sum_{F}\left[2C_{2}\left(\mathcal{R}^{F}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}\right)+\frac{10}{3}C_{2}\left(\mathcal{G}_{\Upsilon}\right)\right]T\left(\mathcal{R}^{F}_{\Upsilon}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{S}\left[4C_{2}\left(\mathcal{R}^{S}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}\right)+\frac{2}{3}C_{2}\left(\mathcal{G}_{\Upsilon}\right)\right]T\left(\mathcal{R}^{S}_{\Upsilon}\right)\,,

(115b)

and the
f_{\Upsilon}(Y_{\mathcal{F}})
are the polynomials to account for the renormalizable Yukawa couplings at the two-loop level.
Here,
C_{2}(\mathcal{G}_{\Upsilon})
is the quadratic Casimir of the gauge group
\mathcal{G}_{\Upsilon}
, and
T(\mathcal{R}^{F}_{\Upsilon})
and
T(\mathcal{R}^{S}_{\Upsilon})
are trace invariants of the chiral fermions in the IR of
\mathcal{R}^{F}_{\Upsilon}
and complex scalars in the IR of
\mathcal{R}^{S}_{\Upsilon}
.
For the
{\rm U}(1)
Abelian groups with charges denoted as
\mathcal{X}^{F/S}
, the one- and two-loop
\beta
coefficients read

\displaystyle b^{\left(1\right)}_{\Upsilon}=\frac{2}{3}\sum_{F}\left(\mathcal{X}^{F}_{\Upsilon}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{S}\left(\mathcal{X}^{S}_{\Upsilon}\right)^{2}\,,

(116a)

\displaystyle b_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}^{(2)}=2\sum_{F}(\mathcal{X}^{F}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}})^{2}\cdot(\mathcal{X}^{F}_{\Upsilon})^{2}+4\sum_{S}(\mathcal{X}^{S}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}})^{2}\cdot(\mathcal{X}^{S}_{\Upsilon})^{2}\,.

(116b)

Below, we first list the minimal set of massless Higgs fields according to the survival hypothesis
[
16
,
34
,
35
,
36
,
37
,
38
]
and the massless fermions between different symmetry breaking stages according to the minimal Higgs VEVs in Eqs. (
22
) and (
33
).
The two-loop RGEs within the minimal
{\rm SU}(8)
setup are therefore derived by using the PyR@TE code
[
39
]
.

)-(

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{,\omega}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{,\dot{\omega}}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{4}\,,\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}}\subset\mathbf{70_{H}}\,.

5.2
RGEs in the WWS symmetry breaking pattern

Between the
v_{441}\leq\mu\leq v_{U}

\mathcal{G}_{441}

\beta
coefficients are identical to Eq. (

), while the matching conditions at
v_{441}
are identical to Eq. (

).

v_{431}\leq\mu\leq v_{441}
, the massless Higgs fields are

6
Summary and outlook

The WSW and the WWS symmetry breaking patterns following the maximally broken
{\rm SU}(8)
theory have been analyzed in both the fermion spectra and the gauge coupling evolutions.
Throughout our analyses of both patterns, we compare our results from what we obtained previously in Refs.
[
17
,
20
,
21
]
.

(i)

Given the
{\rm SU}(8)
chiral fermions in Eq. (
2
), one finds six vectorlike
(\mathbf{5_{F}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{5_{F}}})
pairs and one
(\mathbf{10_{F}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{10_{F}}})
pair in addition to three-generational SM fermions through Georgi’s decomposition rule
[
16
]
.
Both symmetry breaking patterns are found to give rise to massive vectorlike fermions in the spectrum consistent to what we have found along the SWW pattern
[
17
,
20
]
.

(ii)

With the central motivation to address the SM quark/lepton masses through the nonuniversal Yukawa couplings to one unique SM Higgs boson in the spectrum, the SM fermion flavor identifications are elaborated along both the WSW and the SWW pattern.
In particular, we found that the flavor identifications between the first- and second-generational SM fermions are different from those in the SWW pattern, as one can compare the SM flavors in Tables
4
and
7
versus the results in Table
14
.
The flavor identifications are determined by requiring the similar up-type quark mass matrices in all three symmetry breaking patterns, as one can find in Eqs. (
162
), (
192
), and (
A
).
These flavor identifications, together with the down-type quarks, lead to both reasonable SM quark mass hierarchies as well as the observed CKM mixing pattern.
There is only one unique SM Higgs boson predicted in the current minimal model setup, regardless of the specific symmetry breaking pattern.
Hence we do not expect the discovery of any other beyond the SM particle(s) within few
{\rm TeV}
scale at the LHC.

(iii)

There are also observed differences in the SM quark/lepton mass spectra between the (SWW, WSW) and the WWS patterns.
The up-type quark mass matrix along the WWS pattern contains the dimensionless parameters of
(\zeta_{1}\,,\zeta_{3}\,,\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime})
in Eq. (
192
), rather than dimensionless parameters of
(\zeta_{1}\,,\zeta_{2}\,,\dot{\zeta}_{2})
.
For the down-type quark and charged lepton sectors along the WWS pattern, the mass matrices contain the dimensionless parameters of
(\dot{\zeta}_{2}\,,\dot{\zeta}_{3})
.
Correspondingly, the Cabibbo angle is then interpreted as the ratio between two different symmetry breaking scales such that
\lambda=|V_{us}|=\zeta_{23}
.
In the SWW and the WSW patterns, the Cabibbo angle is interpreted as the relative ratio between two
\mathcal{G}_{331}
breaking VEVs in Eq. (
171
).
These differences originate from the Higgs VEVs at the third symmetry breaking stage in the rank-three chiral IRAFFS sector since (i) along the SWW and the WSW patterns, the Higgs VEVs at the third symmetry breaking stage come from two different Higgs components of
(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\subset(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}
and
(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\subset(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}
in Eq. (
20
), and (ii) along the WWS pattern, the Higgs VEVs at the third symmetry breaking stage come from one single Higgs component of
(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\subset(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}
in Eq. (
31
).
Another consequence of the above differences can be found in the suggested benchmark points in Tables
9
and
11
.

(iv)

The gauge coupling evolutions in both patterns are estimated according to the two-loop RGEs based on the suggested intermediate symmetry breaking scales in Eqs. (
76
) and (
113
).
Their behaviors are largely matching with what we have found in the SWW sequence
[
21
]
, where large discrepancies of
|\alpha_{X_{0}}^{-1}-(\alpha_{4S}^{-1}+\alpha_{4W}^{-1})/2|\sim 20
above
\mu\sim 10^{17}\,{\rm GeV}
can be observed in both Figs.
2
and
3
.
Within the field theory context, such large discrepancy could not be reconciled with the one-loop threshold effects
[
40
,
41
,
42
]
.
Altogether, the gauge coupling unification should be achieved in the context of the affine Lie algebra such that

\displaystyle k_{s}\alpha_{4S}(v_{U})=k_{W}\alpha_{4W}(v_{U})=k_{0}\alpha_{X_{0}}(v_{U})=\alpha_{U}(v_{U})\,,

(136)

where
k_{i}
represent the affine levels of each gauge coupling.
To achieve the string unification, it is necessary to consider the constraints of the unitarity and the conformal invariance to the affine
\widehat{{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}}(8)
Lie algebra.
In a sequential work
[
43
]
, we have proved that the unification as in Eq. (
136
) can be achieved with
k_{0}=\frac{1}{4}
, as well as the supersymmetric extension.
The unification scale and gauge coupling therein were suggested to be
v_{U}\approx 8.0\times 10^{17}\,\rm{GeV}
and
\alpha_{U}^{-1}(v_{U})\approx 30.9
.
By using the naive estimation
[
44
,
45
]
of

\displaystyle\tau[p\to\pi^{0}+e^{+}]

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{v_{U}^{4}}{m_{p}^{5}}\,,\quad m_{p}=938\,{\rm MeV}\,,

(137)

purely from the gauge sector contribution, we find a much enhanced lifetime of
\tau[p\to\pi^{0}+e^{+}]\sim 5.6\times 10^{71}\,{\rm yrs}
.
Notice that the nontrivially flavor embedding structure could potentially further enhance this conventional proton decay lifetime.
This means several ongoing experimental probes of the proton decay modes are unlikely to discover such a signal, with the typical exclusion limit of
\sim{\cal O}(10^{34})\,{\rm yrs}
from the Super-Kamiokande
[
46
]
or DUNE Collaboration
[
47
]
.

\displaystyle\bcancel{ ( \mathbf{3}\,, \mathbf{\lx@text@overline{2}}\,, +\frac{1}{6} )_{ \mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime} \oplus( \mathbf{3}\,, \mathbf{1}\,, +\frac{2}{3} )_{ \mathbf{F}} }\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\Big]

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle\Big[(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}\oplus\bcancel{(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,-\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle\bcancel{ ( \mathbf{3}\,, \mathbf{1}\,, -\frac{1}{3})_{ \mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime} }\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\Big]\subset\mathbf{56_{F}}\,.

The fermions that become massive at this stage are further crossed out.
After this stage of symmetry breaking, there are three-generational massless SM fermions together with
23
left-handed massless sterile neutrinos.
5
5

5

The number of residual left-handed massless sterile neutrinos have been precisely obtained through the ‘t Hooft anomaly matching in Ref.
[
17
]
.

The third-generational SM fermions are from the rank-two chiral IRAFFS of
\left[\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\omega}\oplus\mathbf{28_{F}}\right]
, while the first- and second-generational SM fermions are from the rank-three chiral IRAFFS of
\left[\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}}\oplus\mathbf{56_{F}}\right]
.

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,+\frac{3}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}\otimes\langle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\Big]\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\prime\,{\dagger}}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}\otimes\langle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{2M_{\rm pl}m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}}^{2}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\Big]\otimes\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{{\dagger}}\rangle\otimes\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime\prime\,{\dagger}}\rangle+H.c.

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}}{4}\dot{\zeta}_{3}\Big[\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}^{2}}(\underline{d_{L}{d_{R}}^{c}}+\underline{e_{L}{e_{R}}^{c}})+\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}^{2}}(\underline{d_{L}{s_{R}}^{c}}+\underline{\mu_{L}{e_{R}}^{c}})\Big]v_{\rm EW}+H.c.\,,

where the SM quark/lepton components from the
\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}=\dot{1}}
/
\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}=\dot{2}}
correspond to the
({d_{R}}^{c}\,,e_{L})
and
({s_{R}}^{c}\,,\mu_{L})
, respectively.
The other mass terms read

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}\times\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{70_{H}}^{\dagger}\Large(\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\Large)+H.c.

(159)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{4}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}\otimes\langle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]

\displaystyle\otimes

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}\times\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{{\dagger}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\prime\,{\dagger}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{{\dagger}}

\displaystyle\otimes

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\Big]

\displaystyle\otimes

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}\times d_{\mathscr{B}}\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{2M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{{\dagger}}\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\,{\dagger}}\rangle\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime\prime\,{\dagger}}+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}^{\prime}}{2\sqrt{2}M_{\rm pl}m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}}^{2}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}^{\prime}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\Big]\otimes\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime\prime\,{\dagger}}\rangle+H.c.

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}}{4}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\Big[\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}^{2}}(\underline{s_{L}{d_{R}}^{c}}+\underline{e_{L}{\mu_{R}}^{c}})+\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}^{2}}(\underline{s_{L}{s_{R}}^{c}}+\underline{\mu_{L}{\mu_{R}}^{c}})\Big]v_{\rm EW}+H.c.\,.

With the Higgs VEV assignments in (
22c
), one expects the natural propagator masses of

\displaystyle m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}\sim m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}\sim\mathcal{O}(v_{431})\,,

\displaystyle m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}\sim m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}\sim\mathcal{O}(v_{331})\,.

(160)

For convenience, we parametrize the following ratios of

\displaystyle\Delta_{\dot{\omega}}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}^{2}}\,,\quad\Delta_{\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}^{2}}\,.

(161)

B.6
The SM quark/lepton masses and the CKM mixing

For all up-type quarks with
Q_{e}=+\frac{2}{3}
, we write down the following tree-level masses from both the renormalizable Yukawa couplings and the gravity-induced terms in the basis of
\mathcal{U}\equiv(u\,,c\,,t)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&c_{4}\dot{\zeta}_{2}/\sqrt{2}&c_{5}\zeta_{1}/\sqrt{2}\\
0&0&c_{5}\zeta_{2}/\sqrt{2}\\
c_{5}\zeta_{1}/\sqrt{2}&c_{5}\zeta_{2}/\sqrt{2}&Y_{\mathcal{T}}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\approx\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)}+\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(1)}+\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(2)}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&Y_{\mathcal{T}}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&c_{5}\zeta_{1}/\sqrt{2}\\
0&0&0\\
c_{5}\zeta_{1}/\sqrt{2}&0&0\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&c_{4}\dot{\zeta}_{2}/\sqrt{2}&0\\
0&0&c_{5}\zeta_{2}/\sqrt{2}\\
0&c_{5}\zeta_{2}/\sqrt{2}&0\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,,

where we have neglected the
\sim\mathcal{O}(\zeta_{3}\,v_{\rm EW})
terms in the above expansions.
One obvious feature is that the gauge eigenstates of up quark and the charm quark do not obtain tree-level masses through the
d=5
operators with the SM Higgs doublet.
Instead, there are only off-diagonal mass mixing terms in Eqs. (
162
) and (
162
).
Accordingly, we find that

\displaystyle{\rm det}^{\prime}\left[\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)}\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)\,{\dagger}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\,v_{\rm EW}^{2}\Rightarrow m_{t}^{2}\approx\frac{1}{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}\,v_{\rm EW}^{2}\,.

(163)

Here and below, we use the
{\rm det}^{\prime}
to denote the matrix determinant that is equal to the products of all nonzero eigenvalues.
Next, we find the charm quark mass squared of

\displaystyle m_{c}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm det}^{\prime}\left[\Big(\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)}+\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(1)}\Big)\cdot\Big(\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)\,{\dagger}}+\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(1)\,{\dagger}}\Big)\right]\Big/{\rm det}^{\prime}\left[\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)}\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)\,{\dagger}}\right]\approx c_{5}^{4}\frac{\zeta_{1}^{4}}{8Y_{\mathcal{T}}^{2}}\,v_{\rm EW}^{2}\,.

(164)

The up quark mass squared can be similarly obtained by

\displaystyle m_{u}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm det}\left[\Big(\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)}+\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(1)}+\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(2)}\Big)\cdot\Big(\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)\,{\dagger}}+\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(1)\,{\dagger}}+\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(2)\,{\dagger}}\Big)\right]

(165)

\displaystyle\Big/{\rm det}^{\prime}\left[\Big(\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)}+\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(1)}\Big)\cdot\Big(\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(0)\,{\dagger}}+\mathcal{M}_{u}^{(1)\,{\dagger}}\Big)\right]\approx c_{4}^{2}\frac{\zeta_{2}^{2}\dot{\zeta}_{2}^{2}}{4\zeta_{1}^{2}}\,v_{\rm EW}^{2}\,.

To summarize, all SM up-type quark masses are expressed as follows

\displaystyle m_{u}\approx c_{4}\frac{\zeta_{2}\dot{\zeta}_{2}}{2\zeta_{1}}v_{\rm EW}\,,\quad m_{c}\approx c_{5}^{2}\frac{\zeta_{1}^{2}}{2\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{T}}}v_{\rm EW}\,,\quad m_{t}\approx\frac{Y_{\mathcal{T}}}{\sqrt{2}}v_{\rm EW}\,.

(166)

For all down-type quarks with
Q_{e}=-\frac{1}{3}
, we find the following tree-level SM mass matrix

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{M}_{d}\Big)_{3\times 3}\approx\frac{1}{4}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}(2c_{3}+Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}})\dot{\zeta}_{3}&(2c_{3}+Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\zeta_{23}^{-2})\dot{\zeta}_{3}&0\\
(2c_{3}+Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}})\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}&(2c_{3}+Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\zeta_{23}^{-2})\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}&0\\
0&0&Y_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathscr{A}}\zeta_{23}^{-1}\zeta_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,.

For convenience, we parametrize all
\mathcal{G}_{331}
breaking VEVs as follows:

\displaystyle\zeta_{3}=\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}=\frac{\dot{\zeta}_{3}}{\tan\lambda}\,.

(171)

It is straightforward to find the following SM down-type quark masses of

\displaystyle m_{b}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}Y_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathscr{A}}\zeta_{23}^{-1}\zeta_{1}\,v_{\rm EW}\,,

(172a)

\displaystyle m_{s}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}(2c_{3}+Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\zeta_{23}^{-2})\dot{\zeta}_{3}\,v_{\rm EW}\,,

(172b)

\displaystyle m_{d}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}c_{3}\dot{\zeta}_{3}\,v_{\rm EW}\,,

(172c)

from Eq. (
B.6
).

For all charged leptons with
Q_{e}=-1
, their tree-level mass matrix is correlated with the down-type quark mass matrix as

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{M}_{\ell}\Big)_{3\times 3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{M}_{d}^{T}\Big)_{3\times 3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}(2c_{3}+Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}^{\prime})\dot{\zeta}_{3}&(2c_{3}+Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}})\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}&0\\
(2c_{3}+Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\zeta_{23}^{-2})\dot{\zeta}_{3}&(2c_{3}+Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}})\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}&0\\
0&0&Y_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathscr{A}}\zeta_{23}^{-1}\zeta_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,.

Thus, it is straightforward to find the tree-level mass relations of

\displaystyle m_{\tau}=m_{b}\,,\quad m_{\mu}=m_{s}\,,\quad m_{e}=m_{d}\,.

(177)

The second symmetry breaking stage of
\mathcal{G}_{431}\to\mathcal{G}_{421}
along the WWS symmetry breaking pattern can be achieved by Higgs fields of
(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\rm V}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\rm V}
and
(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm VII}\,,\dot{1}}^{\prime}\subset(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm VII}\,,\dot{1}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}\,,\dot{1}}

), as well as their

.
Accordingly, the term of
Y_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\rm V}\mathbf{28_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,{\rm V}}+{\rm H.c.}
leads to the vectorial masses of
(\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\,,\check{\mathfrak{n}}^{\prime}\,,\mathfrak{e}^{\prime}\,,\mathfrak{n}^{\prime})
, and the term of
Y_{\mathcal{D}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\rm VII}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}+{\rm H.c.}
leads to the vectorial masses of
(\mathfrak{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}\,,\check{\mathfrak{n}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\,,\mathfrak{e}^{\prime\prime\prime}\,,\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime\prime})
, respectively.
After integrating out the massive fermions, the remaining massless fermions expressed in terms of the
\mathcal{G}_{421}
IRs are the following:

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\Omega}\oplus\Big[(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\Omega}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\Omega^{\prime}}\Big]\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\Omega}\,,

(178)

\displaystyle\Omega=(\omega\,,\dot{\omega})\,,\quad\omega=(3\,,{\rm VI})\,,\quad\dot{\omega}=(\dot{1}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}\,,\dot{\rm IX})\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{{\rm IV}^{\prime\prime}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{{\rm IV}}\,,\quad(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{{\rm V}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{{\rm V}^{\prime\prime}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\rm V}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\rm VII}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\rm VII}^{\prime\prime}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\rm VII}}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus\Big[\cancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}}\oplus\bcancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle\Big[(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus\bcancel{(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}}\Big]\oplus\cancel{(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}}\subset\mathbf{28_{F}}\,,

\displaystyle\Big[\bcancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}\Big]\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{3}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle\Big[(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\oplus\bcancel{(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}}\oplus(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{3}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle\Big[(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}\Big]\subset\mathbf{56_{F}}\,.

We use the slashes and the back slashes to cross out massive fermions at the first and the second stages, respectively.
From the anomaly-free conditions of
[{\rm SU}(4)_{c}]^{2}\cdot{\rm U}(1)_{X_{2}}=0
,
\left[{\rm SU}(2)_{W}\right]^{2}\cdot{\rm U}(1)_{X_{2}}=0
, and
\left[{\rm U}(1)_{X_{2}}\right]^{3}=0
, we find that one

The third symmetry breaking stage of
\mathcal{G}_{421}\to\mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}
can be achieved by Higgs fields of
(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\rm{VI}\,,3}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\rm{VI}\,,3}
and
\left[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm{VIII}}\,,\dot{2}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm{IX}}}^{\prime}\right]\subset(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm{VIII}}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm{IX}}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm{VIII}}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm{IX}}}
, according to the decompositions in Eqs. (

) and (

), as well as their
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T^{\prime\prime\prime}}
charges in Table

.
Accordingly, the term of
Y_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\rm VI\,,3}\mathbf{28_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\rm VI\,,3}+{\rm H.c.}
leads to the vectorial masses of
(\mathfrak{D}\,,\mathfrak{e}^{\prime\prime}\,,\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime})
, the term of
Y_{\mathcal{D}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\rm{VIII}}\,,\dot{2}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm{VIII}}\,,\dot{2}}+{\rm H.c.}
leads to the vectorial masses of
(\mathfrak{D}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\,,\mathfrak{e}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\,,\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime})
, the term of

leads to the vectorial masses of
(\mathfrak{D}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\,,\mathfrak{e}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\,,\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime})
, and the term of
\frac{c_{4}}{M_{\rm{pl}}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\langle\mathbf{63_{H}}\rangle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm{IX}}}^{{\dagger}}+{\rm H.c.}
leads to the vectorial masses of
(\mathfrak{E}\,,\mathfrak{d}\,,\mathfrak{u}\,,\mathfrak{U})
, respectively.
The remaining massless fermions of the
\mathcal{G}_{\rm SM}
are listed as follows:

\displaystyle\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\Omega}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\Omega}\Big]\oplus\Big[(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\Omega}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\Omega^{\prime}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\Big]\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\Omega}\,,\quad\Omega=(\dot{1}\,,\dot{2}\,,3)\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{{\Omega}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\Omega}\,,\quad\Omega=(\rm VI\,,\dot{\rm VIII}\,,\dot{\rm IX})\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{{\Omega}^{\prime}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\Omega}\,,\quad\Omega=(\rm V\,,\rm VI\,,\dot{\rm VII}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}\,,\dot{\rm IX})\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{{\Omega}^{\prime\prime}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\Omega}\,,\quad\Omega=(\rm IV\,,\rm V\,,\rm VI\,,\dot{\rm VII}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}\,,\dot{\rm IX})\,,

\displaystyle\Big[\xcancel{(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]\oplus\Big[\cancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}}\oplus\bcancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]

\displaystyle\oplus\Big[(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}}\oplus\bcancel{(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}}\Big]\oplus\Big[\cancel{(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}}\Big]\subset\mathbf{28_{F}}\,,

\displaystyle\Big[\bcancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}}\Big]\oplus\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-1)_{\mathbf{F}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\oplus\Big[(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}\oplus\bcancel{(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}}\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}}\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\Big]

\displaystyle\oplus\Big[(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,-\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}}\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\oplus\xcancel{(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\Big]\subset\mathbf{56_{F}}\,.

(179)

The fermions that become massive at this stage are further crossed out.
After this stage of symmetry breaking, there are three-generational massless SM fermions together with 23 left-handed massless sterile neutrinos.
The third-generational SM fermions are from the rank-two chiral IRAFFS of
\left[\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\omega}\oplus\mathbf{28_{F}}\right]
, while the first- and second-generational SM fermions are from the rank-three chiral IRAFFS of
\left[\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}}\oplus\mathbf{56_{F}}\right]
.

(186)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{B}}\left[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{4}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{6}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}\right]\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\mathscr{A}}}{M_{\rm{pl}}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{3}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{4}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{B}}\left[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}\right]

\displaystyle\otimes

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{1}}\times\frac{d_{\mathscr{A}}W_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,\rm{IV}}}{\sqrt{2}M_{\rm{pl}}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{3}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{4}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{B}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}^{\prime}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\oplus

If we consider all three possibilities of the propagator masses, each of them leads to the

\displaystyle\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}\times\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{{\dagger}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{{\dagger}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{{\dagger}}

\displaystyle\otimes\langle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{2}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle+H.c.

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}}{4}\dot{\zeta}_{2}\Big[\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}^{2}}(\underline{s_{L}{d_{R}}^{c}}+\underline{e_{L}{\mu_{R}}^{c}})+\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}^{2}}(\underline{s_{L}{s_{R}}^{c}}+\underline{\mu_{L}{\mu_{R}}^{c}})\Big]v_{\rm EW}+H.c.\,.
